- [ ] `kashrut-n9` (easy, reform) What is the prohibition of a mixture of wool and linen called — a classic example of a chok alongside kashrut? — answer: "Sha'atnez" *Why check:* sha'atnez as an example of a chok; variant spellings.
- [ ] `kashrut-n10` (easy, reform) A member of the beit din asks: "Why do people say that a person's table is like an altar? What does this look like in your home?" *Why check:* a model answer about the table as an altar and the customs of the meal.
- [ ] `kashrut-n11` (medium, reform) A member of the beit din asks: "Is kashrut only a list of animals? What other topics are included in these laws?" *Why check:* a model answer about the laws of the descendants of Noah and the obligation after giyur.
- [ ] `kashrut-n12` (easy, reform) Why is the camel not kosher? — answer: "It chews the cud, but its hooves are not split" *Why check:* the signs of the camel (chewing the cud without split hooves).
- [ ] `kashrut-n13` (easy, reform) Which roe is kosher (with supervision of production)? — answer: "Red roe of the salmon family" *Why check:* the kashrut of red roe under supervision and the non-kosher status of sturgeon roe according to the Orthodox position.
- [ ] `kashrut-n14` (medium, reform) How do the Torah's laws about birds differ from the laws about mammals and fish? — answer: "The Torah does not give signs, but lists the forbidden species" *Why check:* the difference in the laws about birds (a list of forbidden species).
- [ ] `kashrut-n15` (hard, reform) How many unclean species of birds does the Talmud count? — answer: "24" *Why check:* the number 24 of unclean species of birds according to Chullin 63b.
- [ ] `kashrut-n16` (hard, orthodox) Which small creatures in food does the prohibition of insects concern according to halacha? — answer: "Those that are visible to the naked eye" *Why check:* the rule about insects visible to the naked eye — check the wording and the reference.
- [ ] `kashrut-n17` (medium, orthodox) An egg with a blood spot may be used if the spot is small. — answer: false *Why check:* the status of the eggs of kosher birds as pareve.
- [ ] `kashrut-n18` (medium, reform) The dye carmine (E120) is kosher because it is obtained from plants. — answer: false *Why check:* the non-kosher status of carmine E120.
- [ ] `kashrut-n19` (hard, reform) Match the sign from Mishnah Chullin 3:6 with its meaning. — pairs: Bird of prey → According to the Mishnah, not kosher; Hind toe → An extra toe of a kosher bird; Crop → A kosher bird must have one; Stomach lining → It comes off easily by hand *Why check:* criteria of kashrut by groups; locusts — only in communities with a tradition.
- [ ] `kashrut-n20` (easy, reform) Four species of which insect does the Torah permit as food? — answer: "The locust" *Why check:* the four species of locust and the communities that have preserved the tradition.
- [ ] `kashrut-n21` (medium, reform) The Torah names four species of locust as permitted. Why, in most communities, do people still not eat locusts? *Why check:* a model answer about checking vegetables for insects; the method of checking is practice — clarify with a rabbi.
- [ ] `kashrut-n22` (medium, reform) A member of the beit din asks: "Besides meat, which products of animal origin do you check for kashrut, and why?" *Why check:* a model answer about products of non-kosher animals and about honey.
- [ ] `kashrut-n23` (easy, reform) What is cut in shechita? — answer: "The trachea and the esophagus: in livestock, the greater part of both; in a bird, the greater part of one" *Why check:* exactly what is cut in shechita.
- [ ] `kashrut-n24` (easy, reform) What reason for the prohibition of blood does the verse of the Torah itself give? — answer: "The blood is the soul" *Why check:* the basis of the prohibition of blood according to Devarim 12:23.
- [ ] `kashrut-n25` (medium, reform) To which animals does the prohibition of the sciatic nerve (gid ha-nasheh) apply? — answer: "To domestic and wild animals, but not to birds" *Why check:* the extension of the prohibition of gid ha-nasheh to wild animals and its inapplicability to birds.
- [ ] `kashrut-n26` (hard, reform) What is shuman, and what is its status? — answer: "Ordinary fat that permeates the meat — it is permitted" *Why check:* the distinction between chelev and shuman.
- [ ] `kashrut-n27` (hard, orthodox) How may one kasher meat that was not salted and not rinsed within 72 hours after shechita? — answer: "Only by roasting over a fire" *Why check:* the 72-hour rule without salting and kashering only by fire — a numerical detail.
- [ ] `kashrut-n28` (easy, reform) Fish and locusts do not require shechita. — answer: true *Why check:* fish and locusts without shechita.
- [ ] `kashrut-n29` (hard, orthodox) In the strict sense, the term "glatt" refers only to the lungs of cattle. — answer: true *Why check:* the strict meaning of the term "glatt" (only the lungs of cattle).
- [ ] `kashrut-n30` (easy, orthodox) Match the term with its meaning. — pairs: Nikur → Removal of chelev and the sciatic nerve; Melichah → Salting meat to remove the blood; Kisuy ha-dam → Covering the blood of a bird and of game with earth; Tza'ar ba'alei chayim → The prohibition of causing animals unnecessary suffering *Why check:* the definitions of nikur, melichah, kisuy ha-dam, and tza'ar ba'alei chayim.
- [ ] `kashrut-n31` (easy, reform) What is the special Jewish method of slaughter called, without which even a cow or a chicken is forbidden as food? — answer: "Shechita" *Why check:* the term "shechita" and variant spellings.
- [ ] `kashrut-n32` (hard, reform) What is the punishment "cutting off of the soul," which the Torah assigns for deliberate consumption of blood, called? — answer: "Karet" *Why check:* the punishment of karet for blood (Vayikra 7:27).
- [ ] `kashrut-n33` (hard, orthodox) Explain why an animal after a proper shechita may turn out to be a treifah, and what "glatt" means for Sephardim and Ashkenazim. *Why check:* terefot, sirchot, and the difference between the Sephardi and Ashkenazi custom of "glatt" — a question of custom.
- [ ] `kashrut-n34` (easy, reform) What, according to the Shulchan Aruch, is it enough to do after dairy food before eating meat? — answer: "Rinse the mouth, eat something pareve, wash the hands, and change the tablecloth and the utensils" *Why check:* the rules for moving from dairy to meat; the Ashkenazi custom of waiting longer, and a note about Reform.
- [ ] `kashrut-n35` (medium, reform) May two acquaintances eat meat and cheese at one table if there is no divider between them? — answer: "No: a noticeable divider is needed — a separate tablecloth or an object between them" *Why check:* separation of sponges, towels, and drying racks.
- [ ] `kashrut-n36` (medium, reform) How does the Rambam explain why, after meat, one must wait before eating dairy? — answer: "Fibers of meat get stuck between the teeth" *Why check:* attribution of the explanations of the waiting to the Rambam and to Rashi.
- [ ] `kashrut-n37` (hard, orthodox) After which dairy product, according to the stringent custom cited by the Rema, does one wait as long as after meat? — answer: "After hard cheese aged half a year or more" *Why check:* the custom of waiting after hard cheese and the boundary of six months — a numerical detail and a custom.
- [ ] `kashrut-n38` (easy, reform) Marking meat utensils red and dairy utensils blue is required by halacha. — answer: false *Why check:* color marking of utensils as a custom, not a law.
- [ ] `kashrut-n39` (medium, reform) Fish may not be eaten together with meat under the same prohibition as meat with milk. — answer: false *Why check:* the status of pareve cooked in a meat utensil (a question about rice; the nuances of a clean utensil are worth checking).
- [ ] `kashrut-n40` (hard, orthodox) The hours of waiting after meat are counted from the beginning of the meat meal. — answer: false *Why check:* counting the wait from the end of the meat meal.
- [ ] `kashrut-n41` (easy, reform) Match the word with its meaning. — pairs: Basari → Meat (Hebrew); Chalavi → Dairy (Hebrew); Fleishig → Meat (Yiddish); Milchig → Dairy (Yiddish) *Why check:* the terms basari/chalavi/fleishig/milchig.
- [ ] `kashrut-n42` (hard, reform) What was the name of the sage of the Talmud whose account of his father and of himself became the source of the custom of waiting after meat? — answer: "Mar Ukva" *Why check:* Mar Ukva as the source of the custom of waiting (Chullin 105a).
- [ ] `kashrut-n43` (medium, reform) A member of the beit din asks: "How long will you wait after meat, and how did you choose this custom?" *Why check:* a model answer about choosing the custom of waiting (6/3/1 hour) — a question of the community's custom.
- [ ] `kashrut-n44` (easy, conservative) What is the main principle of kashering utensils? — answer: "As it absorbed, so it releases" *Why check:* the principle "as it absorbed, so it releases".
- [ ] `kashrut-n45` (easy, conservative) From which account in the Torah are the laws of kashering utensils derived? — answer: "About the spoils after the war with Midian" *Why check:* the source of kashering in the account of the utensils of Midian.
- [ ] `kashrut-n46` (medium, orthodox) Why is glassware bought from a non-Jew immersed in a mikveh? — answer: "By a decree of the Sages: glass, like metal, can be melted down" *Why check:* the immersion of glass by a decree of the Sages and its rationale.
- [ ] `kashrut-n47` (hard, orthodox) What reason does the lesson give for the prohibition of cheese without Jewish supervision (gevinat akum)? — answer: "Rennet from the stomachs of animals slaughtered in a non-kosher way" *Why check:* the reason for the prohibition of gevinat akum (rennet); there is a note about Masorti.
- [ ] `kashrut-n48` (easy, conservative) Before kashering, utensils are usually cleaned thoroughly and not used for 24 hours. — answer: true *Why check:* the custom of waiting 24 hours before kashering (ben yomo).
- [ ] `kashrut-n49` (hard, orthodox) R. Moshe Feinstein forbade any milk at whose milking no Jew was present, even under strict government supervision. — answer: false *Why check:* the position of R. Moshe Feinstein on milk under government supervision.
- [ ] `kashrut-n50` (hard, orthodox) Match the decree of the Sages with what it concerns. — pairs: Bishul akum → A dish cooked by a non-Jew; Pat akum → Bread baked by a non-Jew at home; Pat palter → Bread from a non-Jewish bakery; Chalav akum → Milk at whose milking no Jew was present *Why check:* the definitions of bishul akum, pat akum, pat palter, and chalav akum.
- [ ] `kashrut-n51` (easy, orthodox) What is the immersion in a mikveh of utensils made or bought from a non-Jew called? — answer: "Tevilat keilim" *Why check:* the term tevilat keilim and the accepted variants.
- [ ] `kashrut-n52` (medium, orthodox) You bought new metal pots and glass cups in an ordinary store. What will you do before the first use? *Why check:* a model answer about the procedure of tevilat keilim and the formula of the blessing.
- [ ] `kashrut-n53` (hard, orthodox) Why did the Sages forbid food prepared by a non-Jew (bishul akum) if all the products in it are kosher? How will you act? *Why check:* a model answer about bishul akum with the Ashkenazi and Sephardi custom — a question of custom.
- [ ] `kashrut-n54` (easy, reform) How does the blessing over wine end? — answer: "...borei pri ha-gafen" *Why check:* the ending of the blessing over wine and the distractor blessings.
- [ ] `kashrut-n55` (easy, reform) Why may an ordinary-looking cookie without a hechsher turn out to be non-kosher? — answer: "It may contain gelatin, whey, carmine, or wine" *Why check:* hidden non-kosher ingredients in ready-made products.
- [ ] `kashrut-n56` (medium, reform) What does the mark "DE" next to a kashrut symbol mean? — answer: "The product was made on dairy equipment" *Why check:* the meaning of the mark DE.
- [ ] `kashrut-n57` (hard, reform) What is "Magen Tzedek"? — answer: "An ethical mark of the Masorti movement in addition to a hechsher" *Why check:* the "Magen Tzedek" initiative in the Masorti movement — a detail about the movement.
- [ ] `kashrut-n58` (easy, reform) A kosher restaurant must display a current kashrut certificate with the date and the name of the organization. — answer: true *Why check:* the requirement of a current teudat kashrut in a restaurant.
- [ ] `kashrut-n59` (hard, orthodox) In Israel, a plant product needs only a hechsher on the ingredients: terumot, ma'aserot, orlah, and shemittah are not checked separately. — answer: false *Why check:* the extension of stam yeinam to juice and grape brandy; the CJLS position differs.
- [ ] `kashrut-n60` (hard, reform) Match the kashrut designation with its meaning. — pairs: OU → Orthodox Union (USA); KLBD → London Beit Din; Badatz → Supervision of a rabbinical court (beit din tzedek); Mehadrin → A higher level of stringency in Israel *Why check:* the meaning of the marks OU, KLBD, Badatz, and mehadrin.
- [ ] `kashrut-n61` (easy, reform) What is the specialist who supervises kashrut in production, in a restaurant, or in a kitchen called? — answer: "Mashgiach" *Why check:* the term "mashgiach" and variant spellings.
- [ ] `kashrut-n62` (easy, reform) A member of the beit din asks: "How do you choose products in an ordinary supermarket?" *Why check:* a model answer about buying products with a hechsher.
- [ ] `kashrut-n63` (hard, reform) How do the approaches of Orthodox, Conservative (Masorti), and Reform Judaism to kashrut differ? *Why check:* a comparison of the three movements, the Pittsburgh Platform of 1885, and the decisions of the CJLS — differences among the movements.
<!-- /sec -->
<!-- sec:05-prazdniki -->
### Holidays

- [ ] `prazdniki-n1` (easy, reform) On what date of the Jewish calendar does Chanukah always fall? — answer: "On 25 Kislev" *Why check:* The date 25 Kislev is named in the lesson directly; the wording should be checked against the community's calendar practice.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n2` (easy, reform) Which mitzvah did the people receive first, while still in Egypt, on the eve of the Exodus? — answer: "To sanctify the coming of the new month" *Why check:* The wording "the first mitzvah" is as in the lesson; the rabbi should confirm that this is how it is taught to the candidate.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n3` (medium, reform) From which month does the Torah count the months, and when does the year number change? — answer: "The months from Nisan, and the year number from 1 Tishrei" *Why check:* The pairing of the two counts is easily confused; let the rabbi check that the answer does not mix them.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n4` (hard, reform) Whom does tradition call the issuer of the fixed calculated calendar? — answer: "Patriarch Hillel II, around 359 CE" *Why check:* The year 359 and the name Hillel II are a precise historical detail of the lesson; it needs to be checked.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n5` (hard, reform) What is the "half" Hallel on Rosh Chodesh? — answer: "Psalms 113–118, but with omissions" *Why check:* The numbers of Psalms 113–118 and the qualification "with omissions" are a precise detail; the rabbi should confirm the wording.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n6` (medium, reform) The familiar names of the months — Nisan, Tishrei, Adar — came from the Babylonian exile. — answer: true *Why check:* A historical formulation about the names of the months; one should make sure that it does not sound more categorical than the lesson.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n7` (hard, orthodox) One who forgot "Ya'aleh ve-Yavo" in the evening Amidah of Rosh Chodesh is obligated to repeat the Amidah. — answer: false *Why check:* The Kitzur's rule about repeating the Amidah and the nighttime sanctification of the month is an Orthodox detail; a rabbi's check is needed.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n8` (hard, reform) Match the "new year" date from the Mishnah with what it was established for. — pairs: 1 Nisan → Kings and festivals; 1 Elul → The tithe of livestock; 1 Tishrei → Years, shemittah, and the Jubilee; 15 Shevat → Trees *Why check:* The four dates and their purposes are an exact place in the Mishnah; the rabbi should check the wordings.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n9` (medium, reform) What is a leap, "pregnant" year called in Hebrew? Two words. — answer: "Shana me'uberet" *Why check:* The transliteration "me'uberet" is spelled in different ways; the rabbi should check the spelling accepted in the community.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n10` (medium, reform) At the interview you are asked: "How do you observe Rosh Chodesh — the semi-festival at home and the prayer in the synagogue?" *Why check:* The answer contains several ritual details of Rosh Chodesh, including a women's custom; a rabbi's check is needed.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n11` (hard, reform) Beit din question: "Name the leap years of the nineteen-year cycle and say when Purim falls in such a year." *Why check:* The numbers of the seven years of the cycle are a precise list; the rabbi must confirm that the candidate will not mix them up.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n12` (easy, reform) What is the Shabbat between Rosh Hashanah and Yom Kippur called? — answer: "Shabbat Shuvah" *Why check:* The name Shabbat Shuvah is short and stable; still, let the rabbi confirm that this is how it is said in the community.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n13` (easy, reform) How many communal prayers are recited on Yom Kippur? — answer: "Five: Maariv, Shacharit, Musaf, Mincha, and Ne'ilah" *Why check:* The list of five services should be checked against the community's siddur: the names are the same, the order of the day may feel different.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n14` (hard, reform) How many shofar sounds, according to custom, are produced during the morning of Rosh Hashanah? — answer: "One hundred sounds during the morning" *Why check:* The number "one hundred" is a custom, not a verse of the Torah; it is important for the rabbi to confirm that the community does indeed count this way.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n15` (hard, reform) How many times at the end of Ne'ilah does one say "The Lord — He is G-d"? — answer: "Seven times" *Why check:* The count of 1, 3, and 7 is easy to mix up; this is a precise liturgical detail, and the rabbi must check it.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n16` (easy, reform) In the month of Elul the shofar is sounded in the synagogue in the mornings, in preparation for the Days of Awe. — answer: true *Why check:* The morning shofar of Elul is a widespread custom of the lesson; one should confirm that this is the practice among you as well.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n17` (medium, reform) Selichot are recited from the end of Elul, and among Sephardim from the very beginning of Elul. — answer: true *Why check:* The Sephardi time for selichot is named apart from the general one; the rabbi should confirm that the general time in the lesson is the end of Elul.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n18` (medium, reform) Kol Nidrei annuls contracts and promises given to other people. — answer: false *Why check:* The boundary of Kol Nidrei is a frequent mistake at the beit din; the wording "does not annul obligations to people" should be confirmed.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n19` (medium, reform) Match the three themes of the Musaf of Rosh Hashanah with their meaning. — pairs: Malkhuyot → The kingship of the Almighty; Zikhronot → Remembrance of the creations and of the covenant; Shofarot → The shofar of Sinai and of the future redemption *Why check:* The three themes of Musaf are named in the lesson; the rabbi should check that the brief definitions do not contradict them.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n20` (medium, reform) What is the last meal before the fast of Yom Kippur called? Two words. — answer: "Se'udah mafseket" *Why check:* The name of the meal is transmitted in different ways; one should check the transliteration accepted in the community.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n21` (hard, orthodox) A member of the beit din asks: "What are kapparot, what other custom of the eve of Yom Kippur do you know, and was there a dispute about these customs?" *Why check:* The dispute of the Rashba, R. Yosef Karo, and the Rema about kapparot is a sensitive custom; the rabbi must check each name and the conclusion.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n22` (easy, reform) What is Sukkot called in the festival prayers? — answer: "Zeman simchateinu, the time of our joy" *Why check:* The name "zeman simchateinu" should not be confused with the names of Pesach and Shavuot; the wording is from the lesson.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n23` (easy, reform) How many days after Yom Kippur does Sukkot begin? — answer: "After five days, on 15 Tishrei" *Why check:* The interval "five days" and the date 15 Tishrei are named directly; a quick check is enough.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n24` (medium, reform) Where, according to the tradition of ushpizin, do the righteous guests come? — answer: "If living guests are received, especially the poor" *Why check:* The ushpizin condition is a moral conclusion of the Zohar tradition in the lesson; the tone of the wording should be shown to the rabbi.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n25` (hard, orthodox) What is the smallest number of walls of a sukkah? — answer: "Three: two full ones and one partial one are valid" *Why check:* The minimum of walls and the allowance of a partial third are precise halacha; a rabbi's check is needed.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n26` (medium, reform) A sukkah may be set up under a spreading tree if the sechach is made correctly. — answer: false *Why check:* The two conditions of sechach are often shortened to one; the rabbi should confirm that both are named correctly.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n27` (medium, reform) In the Diaspora, Simchat Torah falls on the same day as Shemini Atzeret. — answer: false *Why check:* The difference between Israel and the Diaspora and the Reform custom of one day need to be checked separately.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n28` (medium, reform) Match the Sukkot plant species with what it has. — pairs: Etrog → Both taste and fragrance; Lulav → Taste, but no fragrance; Hadasim → Fragrance, but no taste; Aravot → Neither taste nor fragrance *Why check:* The correspondence of taste and fragrance to the four species should be checked against the midrash as it is given in the lesson.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n29` (easy, reform) What are the weekdays within the festival called, when some labors are already permitted? — answer: "Chol ha-mo'ed" *Why check:* The term chol ha-mo'ed is written in different ways; one should confirm the spelling that the community teaches.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n30` (hard, reform) What is the memorial prayer that Ashkenazim recite on Shemini Atzeret called? — answer: "Yizkor" *Why check:* Yizkor is named as an Ashkenazi custom; the rabbi should confirm the name of the prayer and that the question is not about Sephardim.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n31` (medium, reform) A member of the beit din asks: "What happens on Simchat Torah, and why does the reading of the Torah begin again at once?" *Why check:* A practical answer about the sukkah and the sechach; the rabbi should check whether there is extra stringency beyond the lesson.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n32` (hard, reform) Beit din question: "What is special about Hoshana Rabbah, and why is Shemini Atzeret already a different festival?" *Why check:* The dates 21 and 22 Tishrei, seven circuits, and the custom of Yizkor are precise details; a rabbi's check is needed.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n33` (easy, reform) What does the prayer name of Pesach, "zeman cheruteinu," mean? — answer: "The time of our freedom, a remembrance of the Exodus" *Why check:* The translation of "zeman cheruteinu" should not be mixed up with the names of Sukkot and Shavuot.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n34` (medium, reform) Why, during the days of the counting of the omer, are weddings not held and hair not cut? — answer: "In memory of the students of Rabbi Akiva who died then" *Why check:* The reason for the restrictions is named, but which exact days they fall on the lesson leaves to the community's custom — this needs to be stated.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n35` (hard, reform) Within what time must the matzah dough reach the oven so as not to become chametz? — answer: "No longer than eighteen minutes from the water until the baking" *Why check:* The number 18 minutes is the halachic norm of the lesson; the rabbi should confirm that this is also how it is taught in practice.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n36` (hard, orthodox) A person forgot the omer count in the evening and remembered only during the day. How should one count further? — answer: "During the day without a blessing, and further in the evenings — with it" *Why check:* The difference between "forgot in the evening" and "missed a full day" is easy to mix up; both rules of the Kitzur must be checked by the rabbi.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n37` (easy, reform) The four cups at the seder are drunk while leaning on the left side. — answer: true *Why check:* The custom of reclining on the left side should be confirmed: the lesson speaks specifically of the left side.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n38` (medium, conservative) On Pesach, Ashkenazim and Sephardim alike eat rice, legumes, and corn. — answer: false *Why check:* Kitniyot are a dispute of custom and of movements; the question has a separate note for Conservative (Masorti) practice of 2015.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n39` (medium, reform) Match the step of the seder with what is done at it. — pairs: Kadesh → Kiddush over the first cup; Karpas → A vegetable in salt water; Korekh → Bitter herbs with matzah, the custom of Hillel; Barekh → The blessing after the meal, the third cup; Tzafun → The hidden afikoman is eaten *Why check:* The order of the seder steps is stable; the names should be checked against the Haggadah by which the community teaches.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n40` (medium, reform) What is the nighttime study of Torah on the festival of Shavuot called? — answer: "Tikkun Leil Shavuot" *Why check:* The name of the nighttime study is written in different ways; one should confirm the community's variant.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n41` (medium, reform) At the interview you are asked: "What do chametz and matzah say about a person, and not only about the kitchen?" *Why check:* The moral comparison of chametz with pride is an image of the lesson, not a verse; the tone should be shown to the rabbi.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n42` (hard, reform) A member of the beit din asks: "In what way was Shavuot a harvest festival, and in what way did it become the day of the Torah? How will you spend the night?" *Why check:* In one answer are the date, the two days of the Diaspora, bikkurim, and Ruth; the rabbi should check that the layers of meaning are not mixed.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n43` (easy, reform) What does the name of the festival "Chanukah" mean? — answer: "“Dedication,” the renewal of the Temple" *Why check:* The translation of the festival's name is given in the lesson; one should confirm that it is not replaced by folk guesses.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n44` (easy, reform) On which day of Adar is Purim celebrated in most cities? — answer: "14 Adar, on the day after the deliverance" *Why check:* The boundary of 14 and 15 Adar depends on the status of the city; the wording "first of all Jerusalem" should be checked.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n45` (medium, reform) From what calamity did Chanukah, according to the lesson, save the people — unlike Purim? — answer: "From spiritual destruction and the desecration of the Temple" *Why check:* The contrast "spiritual / physical" is the lesson's wording; one should make sure that it does not oversimplify history.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n46` (hard, reform) On the Friday of Chanukah, when is the chanukiah lit relative to the Shabbat candles? — answer: "Before the Shabbat candles, and the candles are taken longer" *Why check:* The order of Friday and of the departure of Shabbat is easy to confuse; both rules of the lesson must be checked by the rabbi.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n47` (hard, reform) How long must the Chanukah lights burn after the stars appear? — answer: "No less than half an hour" *Why check:* Half an hour after the stars is a precise time span; the rabbi should confirm that this is how one is taught to light.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n48` (medium, reform) Chanukah and Purim are commanded in the Five Books of the Torah, and therefore work on these days is forbidden, as on Shabbat. — answer: false *Why check:* The status of the books of the Maccabees relative to the Tanakh is named in the lesson; the wording should be shown to the rabbi.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n49` (hard, reform) To reach, on Purim, a drunkenness that harms a person is a fulfillment of the mitzvah. — answer: false *Why check:* The boundary between the words of the Talmud and the Rema's qualification is a disputed practice; the rabbi must confirm the wording.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n50` (hard, reform) Match the letter of the sevivon outside Israel with a word of the phrase "Nes gadol haya sham." — pairs: Nun → Nes — miracle; Gimel → Gadol — great; Hei → Haya — was; Shin → Sham — there *Why check:* The letters of the sevivon and the Israeli replacement of shin with pei are a custom; the question is limited to the Diaspora, but the rabbi should check the decoding.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n51` (easy, reform) What is the money customarily given to children on Chanukah called? — answer: "Chanukah gelt" *Why check:* The word "gelt" came from Yiddish; one should confirm that such an answer is accepted.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n52` (medium, reform) Explain to a child why Chanukah lasts eight days and what the jug of oil has to do with it. *Why check:* The story of the oil is talmudic, not from the Five Books of the Torah; one should check that the answer does not hide this.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n53` (medium, reform) A member of the beit din asks: "How will you hear the Megillah on Purim, and who may read it for the community?" *Why check:* The measure of wine on Purim is something the rabbi must check, together with the minimum of gifts.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n54` (easy, reform) How long do the minor fasts last, for example 17 Tammuz and 10 Tevet? — answer: "From dawn until the stars come out on the same day" *Why check:* The boundary of a minor fast is dawn, not sunset; one should confirm that the answer is not confused with Tisha B'Av.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n55` (easy, reform) What happens in Israel at ten in the morning on Yom HaShoah? — answer: "A siren sounds, and the whole country stands still" *Why check:* The siren at 10 a.m. is a state practice described in the lesson; the wording should be checked.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n56` (hard, reform) When is Yom Yerushalayim observed, and what is remembered? — answer: "28 Iyar — the unification of Jerusalem in 1967" *Why check:* The date 28 Iyar and the year 1967 are precise information; the rabbi should confirm them from the lesson.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n57` (hard, orthodox) If the fast of 17 Tammuz falls on Shabbat, to which day is it postponed? — answer: "To the nearest Sunday" *Why check:* Postponements of fasts differ; Thursday applies only to the Fast of Esther. The numbers and the days must be checked by the rabbi.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n58` (easy, reform) The prophet Zekharyah says that four fasts will in the future become days of joy. — answer: true *Why check:* Zekharyah's promise of future joy should be checked against the verse, without adding one's own time frames.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n59` (hard, orthodox) Under the calendar in use, the fast of 10 Tevet sometimes falls on Shabbat. — answer: false *Why check:* The statement that 10 Tevet never falls on Shabbat is a calendar matter; a rabbi must confirm it.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n60` (medium, reform) Among Ashkenazim, in the "Nine Days," from 1 Av, one does not eat meat and does not drink wine, except on Shabbat. — answer: true *Why check:* This is the Ashkenazi custom of the "Nine Days"; the Sephardi boundary in the lesson is different, and the rabbi must check both qualifications.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n61` (hard, reform) Match the name with the event remembered on a fast or on Tisha B'Av. — pairs: Gedalyah ben Achikam → Governor of Judea after the First Temple; Yishmael ben Netanyah → Killed Gedalyah, and the people fled to Egypt; Apostomus → Burned a Torah scroll on 17 Tammuz; Bar Kokhba → His fortress of Beitar fell; Nebuchadnezzar → Began the siege of Jerusalem on 10 Tevet *Why check:* Five pairs of names and calamities are precise identifications; the rabbi must check each one.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n62` (medium, reform) What is the Hebrew name for the "three weeks" of mourning from 17 Tammuz to 9 Av? — answer: "Bein ha-metzarim" *Why check:* The transliteration "bein ha-metzarim" varies; one should confirm the community's spelling.
- [ ] `prazdniki-n63` (hard, orthodox) A member of the beit din asks: "How does the evening of 9 Av proceed in the synagogue, and which restrictions of the major fast do you take upon yourself?" *Why check:* The answer contains the Orthodox restrictions of Tisha B'Av from the Kitzur; the rabbi must check each one.
<!-- /sec -->
<!-- sec:06-semya -->
### Life cycle and family

- [ ] `semya-n1` (easy, reform) At what age did Avraham enter the covenant of circumcision? — answer: “When he turned ninety-nine” *Why check:* Avraham’s age is a precise detail of Bereshit 17; verify the wording.
- [ ] `semya-n2` (easy, reform) If a boy is born on Shabbat, on which day does his brit milah fall? — answer: “On the next Shabbat, on the eighth day” *Why check:* the count of the eighth day when the birth is on Shabbat is a halachic detail.
- [ ] `semya-n3` (medium, reform) Why does the father hand the mohel the knife before the circumcision? — answer: “Because the mohel acts as the father’s messenger” *Why check:* the knife and the mohel’s status as the father’s messenger are the rule of Kitzur Shulchan Aruch 163:1.
- [ ] `semya-n4` (hard, conservative) What question does the kohen ask the father at the redemption of the firstborn? — answer: ““What do you prefer: the firstborn, or five shekels?”” *Why check:* the kohen’s verbatim question and the five shekels; Conservative level.
- [ ] `semya-n5` (easy, reform) Right after the circumcision, the child is wished entry into the Torah, under the chuppah, and into good deeds. — answer: true *Why check:* the formula of the wish after brit milah, from the siddur.
- [ ] `semya-n6` (medium, reform) Shalom zachar is a Sephardi custom of gathering in the newborn’s home on the night after the circumcision. — answer: false *Why check:* the custom: shalom zachar is Ashkenazi, and on the night before, not Sephardi.
- [ ] `semya-n7` (easy, reform) Match the participant in, or the custom of, brit milah with his or its role. — pairs: The father’s blessing → on bringing the son into the covenant of Avraham; Sandak → the honored guest with the infant on his knees; Seudat mitzvah → the meal in honor of the mitzvah of circumcision; Shalom zachar → an Ashkenazi gathering on the night before *Why check:* the roles of the father, the sandak, the seudat mitzvah, and shalom zachar — verify the wording.
- [ ] `semya-n8` (hard, conservative) How many silver shekels does the father give the kohen at the redemption of the firstborn? — answer: “five shekels” *Why check:* the number: five silver shekels, Bamidbar 18:16; Conservative level.
- [ ] `semya-n9` (hard, conservative) The father never redeemed the firstborn. On whom does this obligation remain when the son grows up? *Why check:* the order if the father did not redeem him: the beit din, then the firstborn himself; Kitzur 164:8.
- [ ] `semya-n10` (easy, reform) What does the word “bat mitzvah” literally mean? — answer: “Daughter of the commandment and her personal duty” *Why check:* the literal meaning of “bat mitzvah”.
- [ ] `semya-n11` (medium, reform) According to Chabad custom, when does a boy begin to put on tefillin? — answer: “Two months before the Jewish date of birth” *Why check:* the Chabad custom was corrected to “two months” (the lesson had said “a month”; in many Ashkenazi communities it is about a month); the lesson text was corrected as well — check
- [ ] `semya-n12` (hard, orthodox) Why does the Shulchan Aruch not call a woman to the Torah in a men’s minyan? — answer: “Because they safeguard the dignity of the community” *Why check:* the source is Shulchan Aruch, Orach Chaim 282:3, and the distinction of an Orthodox minyan.
- [ ] `semya-n13` (easy, reform) What does a young man enter upon reaching bar mitzvah? — answer: “Into the body of the community’s minyan” *Why check:* entry into the minyan from bar mitzvah.
- [ ] `semya-n14` (easy, reform) Until bar mitzvah, the parents are responsible for the child’s conduct before the Almighty. — answer: true *Why check:* until the age of majority, the parents are responsible.
- [ ] `semya-n15` (medium, reform) Exempting women from time-bound mitzvot means their spiritual inequality. — answer: false *Why check:* exemption from time-bound mitzvot is a legal category, not inequality; Mishnah Kiddushin 1:7.
- [ ] `semya-n16` (easy, reform) Match the sign of the age of mitzvot with what stands behind it. — pairs: Ten years → the time to begin the Mishnah; Derashah → a short speech about the portion that was read; Tallit → girls put it on in Masorti and Reform; Candles and tzedakah → a sign of a girl’s coming of age in a traditional home *Why check:* the Avot scale, derashah, tallit in Masorti and Reform, candles in a traditional home.
- [ ] `semya-n17` (hard, reform) What is the Ashkenazi father’s blessing on the day of bar mitzvah called? — answer: “Baruch she-petarani” *Why check:* the exact name of the Ashkenazi father’s blessing.
- [ ] `semya-n18` (hard, reform) You are called to the Torah for the first time. Which blessings do you recite before and after the reading, and what do you acknowledge by them? *Why check:* the literal meaning of the two aliyah blessings according to the siddur.
- [ ] `semya-n19` (easy, reform) Which phrase of the Torah says that a person should not remain alone? — answer: ““It is not good for a person to be alone”” *Why check:* the verse Bereshit 2:18.
- [ ] `semya-n20` (medium, reform) Which of the three methods of kiddushin named by the Mishnah is alive in today’s rite? — answer: “The transfer of value, usually one’s own ring” *Why check:* which of the three methods of Mishnah Kiddushin 1:1 remains in the rite.
- [ ] `semya-n21` (hard, reform) What basic ketubah sum does the Mishnah assign to a widow? — answer: “A sum of one hundred zuz” *Why check:* the number: one hundred zuz for a widow, Mishnah Ketubot 1:2.
- [ ] `semya-n22` (easy, reform) Whose must the ring be with which the groom betroths the bride? — answer: “A ring that belongs to the groom personally” *Why check:* the ring must belong to the groom personally.
- [ ] `semya-n23` (easy, reform) The first commandment spoken to people, back in Eden, is “be fruitful and multiply.” — answer: true *Why check:* Bereshit 1:28 as the first commandment to people.
- [ ] `semya-n24` (medium, reform) Under the order now in force, a full year must be waited between kiddushin and nissuin. — answer: false *Why check:* the ancient interval of up to a year, and today’s single ceremony.
- [ ] `semya-n25` (easy, reform) Match each stage on the way to the chuppah with its meaning. — pairs: Shidduch → getting to know the person before the engagement; Erusin → the old name of betrothal; Nissuin → the second act of marriage, under the canopy; Arayot → forbidden degrees of blood kinship *Why check:* shidduch, erusin, nissuin, and arayot.
- [ ] `semya-n26` (hard, reform) What is the remembrance of the destruction of the Temple called, at the moment when a glass is broken at a wedding? — answer: “zecher le-churban” *Why check:* the term zecher le-churban and Berachot 31a.
- [ ] `semya-n27` (hard, orthodox) You are a man after giyur; the bride is a kohen’s daughter. What does Orthodox halacha say about this marriage? *Why check:* the distinction between a ger and a kohen’s daughter, and a kohen and a woman who is a ger; where the movements diverge.
- [ ] `semya-n28` (easy, reform) In what words does Pirkei Avot speak of the hospitality of a home? — answer: “Keep the house wide open” *Why check:* Pirkei Avot 1:5 on an open house.
- [ ] `semya-n29` (medium, reform) Whom, according to Yevamot 62b, must a husband honor more than himself? — answer: “His own wife” *Why check:* Yevamot 62b: honor one’s wife more than oneself.
- [ ] `semya-n30` (easy, reform) Which verse does the lesson call the foundation of honoring parents? — answer: ““Each of you, honor your mother and your father”” *Why check:* Vayikra 19:3 as the foundation of honoring parents.
- [ ] `semya-n31` (hard, orthodox) How often does the Kitzur Shulchan Aruch command that the parchment of the mezuzah be checked? — answer: “Twice in the course of a seven-year period” *Why check:* the number: checking the mezuzah twice in a seven-year period, Kitzur chapter 11.
- [ ] `semya-n32` (easy, reform) According to Bava Metzia 59a, blessing is found in a home only on account of the wife. — answer: true *Why check:* Bava Metzia 59a on blessing on account of the wife.
- [ ] `semya-n33` (medium, reform) Shalom bayit is kept like this: one stays silent about every hurt and does not ask forgiveness aloud. — answer: false *Why check:* shalom bayit is not silence and not a refusal to apologize.
- [ ] `semya-n34` (hard, orthodox) Which Name of the Almighty is written on the back of the mezuzah scroll? — answer: “Shaddai” *Why check:* the Name Shaddai on the back of the mezuzah; an Orthodox detail of chapter 11.
- [ ] `semya-n35` (easy, reform) How, on your Friday evening, does Shabbat gather the family at the table? *Why check:* Shabbat at home: candles, kiddush, three meals, the blessing of the children.
- [ ] `semya-n36` (hard, reform) To whom did the sages first of all apply Yechezkel’s “little sanctuary,” and what then remains for an ordinary home? *Why check:* Megillah 29a: the “little sanctuary” is first about synagogues and study halls.
- [ ] `semya-n37` (easy, orthodox) What is the Talmudic tractate on the laws of niddah called? — answer: “Tractate Niddah” *Why check:* the name of Tractate Niddah.
- [ ] `semya-n38` (hard, orthodox) According to the widespread custom of Ashkenazim and Sephardim, the count of clean days is not begun earlier than which day? — answer: “Earlier than the fifth day from the start of the period” *Why check:* the custom of Ashkenazim and Sephardim: not earlier than the fifth day; the number.
- [ ] `semya-n39` (medium, orthodox) What do the harchakot between spouses include on the days of niddah? — answer: “No sleeping together, no touching, and no passing from hand to hand” *Why check:* what the harchakot consist of: sleep, touch, passing from hand to hand.
- [ ] `semya-n40` (easy, orthodox) Immersion in the mikveh is performed on the evening of the seventh clean day. — answer: true *Why check:* the evening of the seventh clean day; for Masorti and Reform the obligation is different.
- [ ] `semya-n41` (easy, orthodox) At tevilah a woman immerses seven times in a row with the whole family present. — answer: false *Why check:* one immersion in privacy, with only the attendant.
- [ ] `semya-n42` (easy, orthodox) Match the image of family purity with how the lesson explains it. — pairs: Living water → a spring, or rain in the required measure; The attendant → only she sees the immersion itself; The image of the womb → this is how tradition compares the mikveh; The tractates of Taharot → purity was wider than the bedroom alone *Why check:* living water, the attendant, the image of the womb, the tractates of Taharot.
- [ ] `semya-n43` (hard, orthodox) What is the immersion of new vessels in the mikveh called? — answer: “tevilat kelim” *Why check:* the term tevilat kelim.
- [ ] `semya-n44` (medium, orthodox) On the eve of the chuppah the bride goes to the mikveh. Why is this needed, and what does this evening open in family life? *Why check:* the bride’s mikveh and its obligation in Orthodoxy; differences among the movements.
- [ ] `semya-n45` (hard, orthodox) Where, besides married life, did tradition know tum'ah and taharah, and what of this remained after the Temple? *Why check:* the scope of tum'ah before the Temple, and what remained in the family.
- [ ] `semya-n46` (easy, reform) Who, according to the rules, writes the divorce scroll? — answer: “A specially trained scribe — the sofer” *Why check:* only a sofer writes a get.
- [ ] `semya-n47` (easy, reform) Which formula makes up the essential core of the get? — answer: ““Behold, you are permitted to any man”” *Why check:* the formula “permitted to any man”.
- [ ] `semya-n48` (hard, conservative) In what case does the Torah lay upon the brother of the deceased the obligation of yibbum? — answer: “If a person died without leaving children” *Why check:* yibbum upon a death without children; Reform abolished both rites.
- [ ] `semya-n49` (medium, reform) What does the Lieberman clause, with which Masorti supplements the ketubah, obligate in advance? — answer: “To submit to the decision of the beit din if the marriage comes apart” *Why check:* the Lieberman clause from 1953 and the name Saul Lieberman.
- [ ] `semya-n50` (medium, reform) An error in a name, a date, or a word does not make a get invalid. — answer: false *Why check:* an error in a name, a date, or a word can invalidate a get.
- [ ] `semya-n51` (easy, reform) After a get, a divorced woman becomes forbidden for marriage to a kohen. — answer: true *Why check:* a divorced woman is forbidden to a kohen, Vayikra 21:7.
- [ ] `semya-n52` (easy, reform) Match the rule of divorce with its content. — pairs: From hand to hand → this is how the scroll is handed over before two witnesses; Refusal without grounds → only then does the beit din intervene; Does not feed and humiliates → the wife is entitled to ask for a divorce through the court; The mamzer is not at fault → the weight of the status, and not the person’s guilt *Why check:* transfer from hand to hand, voluntariness, the wife’s right, the mamzer’s innocence.
- [ ] `semya-n53` (medium, reform) What does the Torah call the divorce scroll? — answer: “sefer keritut” *Why check:* the Torah’s term sefer keritut, Devarim 24:1.
- [ ] `semya-n54` (hard, reform) You live in the diaspora, and a marriage will have to be brought to an end. How does handing over the get differ from the state’s decision, and why might a Reform rabbi still recommend the scroll? *Why check:* the diaspora, the get, and civil divorce; the advice of Reform rabbis.
- [ ] `semya-n55` (easy, reform) Who performs taharah and clothes the deceased in tachrichim? — answer: “Volunteers of the chevra kadisha” *Why check:* the chevra kadisha performs taharah and tachrichim.
- [ ] `semya-n56` (hard, reform) What is the time before the funeral called, when the relatives are busy only with the burial? — answer: “Aninut” *Why check:* the term aninut — the time before the funeral.
- [ ] `semya-n57` (medium, reform) What is placed on the grave instead of flowers? — answer: “A small stone, and good deeds in place of the deceased” *Why check:* the custom: a small stone instead of flowers.
- [ ] `semya-n58` (easy, reform) The Kaddish is composed in Aramaic, and it has no words about death. — answer: true *Why check:* the Kaddish is in Aramaic and without words about death.
- [ ] `semya-n59` (hard, reform) A funeral may be postponed for the convenience of distant acquaintances. — answer: false *Why check:* when a postponement of the funeral is permitted, and when it is not.
- [ ] `semya-n60` (easy, reform) Match the custom of farewell with its meaning. — pairs: Taharah → washing the body before burial; Keriah → the rending of the relatives’ clothing at the funeral; Vidui → words of repentance at the bedside; The house of mourning → there the Kaddish is recited the first week *Why check:* taharah, keriah, vidui, and the Kaddish at home during the first week.
- [ ] `semya-n61` (hard, reform) What is the meal of consolation after the return from the cemetery called? — answer: “seudat ha-avra'ah” *Why check:* the name seudat ha-avra'ah — check the accepted vocalization.
- [ ] `semya-n62` (medium, reform) Why is the first yahrzeit treated with special care? Rely on the words of the sages. *Why check:* Moed Katan 27b and the twelve months until the first yahrzeit.
- [ ] `semya-n63` (easy, reform) With which words of Iyov will you meet the death of someone close, and what, according to the lesson, remains after a person? *Why check:* Iyov 1:21 and Kohelet 12:7 in the answer about death.
<!-- /sec -->
<!-- sec:07-etika -->
### Ethics

- [ ] `etika-n1` (easy, reform) To whom does the first teaching of Pirkei Avot belong — about the three pillars on which the world stands? — answer: “Shimon HaTzaddik” *Why check:* Shimon HaTzaddik is the author of the first teaching (1:2), not of mishnah 1:1; check the wording “high priest”.
- [ ] `etika-n2` (easy, reform) What, according to Pirkei Avot 2:5, must a person do “in a place where there are no people”? — answer: “Try to be a person” *Why check:* check the accuracy of the sense of the saying 2:5, “in a place where there are no people — try to be a person”.
- [ ] `etika-n3` (medium, reform) Why does Pirkei Avot open with the chain of the Torah’s transmission, and not at once with a teaching? — answer: “To show that the ethics of Avot are part of the single chain of the Torah’s transmission” *Why check:* the interpretive conclusion about the purpose of the chain of transmission at the beginning of the tractate — check that it agrees with the commentators.
- [ ] `etika-n4` (hard, reform) From which verse does Ben Zoma (Avot 4:1) derive that the strong one is not the brave man, but the long-patient one who masters himself? — answer: “Mishlei 16:32” *Why check:* the source of the verse Mishlei 16:32 in Ben Zoma — compare with the classical commentary.
- [ ] `etika-n5` (easy, reform) Who, according to tradition, headed the Great Assembly — the council with which the chain of the Torah’s transmission in Avot 1:1 comes to an end? — answer: “The prophet Ezra” *Why check:* the tradition that the prophet Ezra stood at the head of the Great Assembly — check the wording.
- [ ] `etika-n6` (easy, reform) According to Pirkei Avot 2:5, an impatient person will not be able to teach another. — answer: true *Why check:* the wording of 2:5 about the impatient one, who “will not teach”.
- [ ] `etika-n7` (easy, reform) According to Ben Zoma (Avot 4:1), rich is the one who has the most property. — answer: false *Why check:* a check of the understanding of the saying 4:1 “who is rich?” — a simple question; the wording should be compared.
- [ ] `etika-n8` (easy, reform) Match the three pillars of the world (Avot 1:2) with their meaning. — pairs: Torah → learning and knowledge; Avodah → service in the Temple, and in a later understanding — prayer; Gemilut chasadim → good deeds out of love: to visit the sick, to welcome a guest, to accompany the deceased *Why check:* the interpretation of the three pillars of 1:2 (avodah = service/prayer) — compare with accepted tradition.
- [ ] `etika-n9` (hard, reform) What is the sixth chapter of Pirkei Avot called, added to the tractate later from other sources? — answer: “Kinyan Torah” *Why check:* a fact about the makeup of the tractate (the 6th chapter, “Kinyan Torah”) — the source is in the lesson’s introduction, not in the list of quotations.
- [ ] `etika-n10` (medium, reform) Pirkei Avot 2:5 says that a shy person will not learn, and an impatient person will not teach. How will you walk the long path of learning after giyur, and what place will patience toward yourself and toward your teachers hold in it? *Why check:* an open personal question about patience in study — the ethical fineness of the wording should be assessed.
- [ ] `etika-n11` (easy, reform) What broader principle did Ben Azzai, a contemporary of Rabbi Akiva, set beside the commandment “love your fellow”? — answer: ““A human being is the image of G-d” (Bereshit 5:1)” *Why check:* Ben Azzai’s principle “a human being is the image of G-d” (Bereshit 5:1) — check the reference.
- [ ] `etika-n12` (medium, reform) What is the practical wisdom of Hillel’s “negative formulation” — “do not do to your fellow what is hateful to you”? — answer: “It is easier to agree on what may not be inflicted than on what each person considers a good” *Why check:* the interpretation of Hillel’s answer in Shabbat 31a — compare with the lesson and the classical sources.
- [ ] `etika-n13` (easy, reform) What everyday example of revenge does Rashi give for the words “do not take revenge” (Vayikra 19:18)? — answer: “A neighbor refused the loan of a sickle — and the next day you refuse him an axe” *Why check:* Rashi’s example of the sickle and the axe — check the accuracy of the retelling.
- [ ] `etika-n14` (hard, reform) What, according to the Talmud (Bava Metzia 58b), is it forbidden to say to a person who has repented of his sins? — answer: ““Remember your former deeds”” *Why check:* the reading of Bava Metzia 58b about the penitent — check the quotation “remember your former deeds”.
- [ ] `etika-n15` (medium, reform) How, according to the lesson, does the Torah propose that one relate to the need of the ger, the widow, and the orphan? — answer: “To recognize oneself in another’s situation, and not to pity from above” *Why check:* the generalization “not to pity from above, but to recognize oneself” — check it as a conclusion of the lesson.
- [ ] `etika-n16` (easy, reform) The Torah requires one to love one’s fellow more than oneself. — answer: false *Why check:* the boundary of the formula “as oneself” — a standard question; verify the wording.
- [ ] `etika-n17` (easy, reform) According to Bava Metzia 58b, it is forbidden to remind a sick person of his illness. — answer: true *Why check:* the extension of the prohibition of BM 58b to the sick — compare with the source.
- [ ] `etika-n18` (hard, reform) Match the verses about the ger with their content. — pairs: “And you shall love the ger, for you were gerim in the land of Egypt” → Devarim 10:19; “Like a native (ezrach) among you shall the ger be for you” → Vayikra 19:34; “And do not oppress the ger” → Shemot 22:20 *Why check:* a check of the verses about the ger (Devarim 10:19; Vayikra 19:34; Shemot 22:20) — accuracy of the references.
- [ ] `etika-n19` (hard, reform) What does the lesson call grudge-bearing — when a person does lend, but with the reproach “I am not like you”? — answer: “netirah” *Why check:* the term “netirah” in Rashi on Vayikra 19:18 — compare with the accepted vocalization.
- [ ] `etika-n20` (medium, reform) Vayikra 19:18 forbids taking revenge and nursing a grudge. Tell of a situation in which it was hard for you to give up keeping a score against a person, and how you understand the commandment “to break the chain of hurts”. *Why check:* an open question about revenge and grudge-bearing — check whether the personal framing is acceptable.
- [ ] `etika-n21` (medium, reform) The stories of Shabbat 31a teach that “a person’s first impression of Jewishness comes from Jews”. How, once you have become a Jew, will you welcome newcomers and gerim in the community? *Why check:* the application of Shabbat 31a to welcoming newcomers — the pedagogical accuracy should be assessed.
- [ ] `etika-n22` (easy, reform) What measure of tzedakah does the Talmud (Bava Batra 9a) give? — answer: ““Tzedakah equals all the mitzvot taken together”” *Why check:* the standard quotation of Bava Batra 9a — verify the wording.
- [ ] `etika-n23` (easy, reform) What, according to Mishlei 21:3, is more pleasing to the Almighty than a sacrifice? — answer: “To do righteousness (tzedakah) and justice” *Why check:* the verse Mishlei 21:3 — check the accuracy of the quotation.
- [ ] `etika-n24` (medium, reform) How does the arrangement of pe'ah, shikhechah, and leket preserve the dignity of the poor person? — answer: “The poor person himself gathers what is due to him — he works, and does not wait for a handout” *Why check:* the conclusion about dignity from the system of pe'ah/shikhechah/leket — compare with the lesson.
- [ ] `etika-n25` (hard, orthodox) What order of priority in tzedakah does the Kitzur Shulchan Aruch (34:6) set? — answer: “Relatives and members of the household — before the poor of one’s own city, and those — before the poor of other cities” *Why check:* HALACHA: the order of priority in tzedakah according to KSA 34:6 (relatives → city → other cities) — check the accuracy and minLevel orthodox.
- [ ] `etika-n26` (hard, reform) According to the Kitzur Shulchan Aruch 34:4, one should give to tzedakah no more than a fifth of one’s property, so as not to fall into need oneself. — answer: true *Why check:* NUMBER and DIFFERENCE OF MOVEMENTS: the limit of a fifth (KSA 34:4) + differs for Reform — must be checked.
- [ ] `etika-n27` (easy, reform) According to the Talmud (Sotah 14a), mercy is cultivated by imitating G-d: as He visits the sick and comforts the mourners, so does a person. — answer: true *Why check:* Sotah 14a on imitating G-d — verify the wording.
- [ ] `etika-n28` (hard, reform) Match the descriptions with the Rambam’s levels of tzedakah (Matnot Aniyim, ch. 10). — pairs: The sages secretly placed coins in the doors of the poor → the giver knows to whom he gives, and the receiver does not know; Coins were placed in a cloth on the shoulder → the receiver knows from whom he takes, and the giver does not know; To give into the hand of a person in need after a request → the sixth level (below “before a request”) *Why check:* a check of the Rambam’s levels 10:9–12 (coins in the doors, a cloth on the shoulder, “after a request”).
- [ ] `etika-n29` (hard, reform) What is the tithe of the poor of the third year of the seven-year cycle called (Devarim 26:12)? — answer: “Maaser ani” *Why check:* the term “maaser ani” and Devarim 26:12 — check the Hebrew form and the verse reference.
- [ ] `etika-n30` (easy, reform) What is the mitzvah of visiting the sick called? — answer: “bikur cholim” *Why check:* the term “bikur cholim” and the basis Sotah 14a / Bereshit 18:1 — standard; verify the wording.
- [ ] `etika-n31` (medium, reform) What is gemilut chasadim, and how does it differ from tzedakah? What kinds of chesed are you prepared to do in your community? *Why check:* an open question about gemilut chasadim — the completeness of the distinction from tzedakah should be assessed.
- [ ] `etika-n32` (medium, reform) Why is giving in Judaism called not alms, but tzedakah — “righteousness”? How does this change the giver’s attitude? *Why check:* an open question about the essence of the word “tzedakah” — check the etymology of “tzedek”.
- [ ] `etika-n33` (easy, reform) Which verse of the book of Mishlei gives the human word exceptional weight? — answer: ““Death and life are in the power of the tongue” (Mishlei 18:21)” *Why check:* the verse Mishlei 18:21 — verify the wording.
- [ ] `etika-n34` (hard, reform) By what method of interpretation does the Talmud in Arakhin 15b equate lashon hara with idolatry, forbidden relations, and murder? — answer: “The method of “gezeirah shavah” — interpretation by the shared word “great”” *Why check:* the term “gezeirah shavah” and its application in Arakhin 15b — check the accuracy.
- [ ] `etika-n35` (hard, reform) What is NOT included in the strict conditions under which, according to the classical authorities, speech about a person is permitted in order to prevent harm? — answer: “The consent of the majority of the community to such a conversation” *Why check:* the four conditions of permitted speech according to the Chafetz Chaim — compare with the classical code.
- [ ] `etika-n36` (easy, reform) The Torah’s prohibition of lying is narrower than the prohibition of evil speech. — answer: false *Why check:* “the prohibition of lying is broader than the prohibition of evil speech” — verify the lesson’s wording (Shemot 23:7; Vayikra 19:11).
- [ ] `etika-n37` (easy, reform) According to the Kitzur Shulchan Aruch 63:1, deception in price is weightier than a verbal hurt, because money is lost through it. — answer: false *Why check:* KSA 63:1 — the comparison of the gravity of a verbal hurt and a monetary hurt; check the accuracy.
- [ ] `etika-n38` (hard, reform) The Chafetz Chaim also wrote the book “Ahavat Chesed” — on the love of goodness and interest-free loans. — answer: true *Why check:* the fact about the book “Ahavat Chesed” — compare with the biography of the Chafetz Chaim.
- [ ] `etika-n39` (hard, reform) Match the wordings with the verses from which they are taken. — pairs: “Death and life are in the power of the tongue” → Mishlei 18:21; “Keep away from a lie” → Shemot 23:7; “Do not lie to one another” → Vayikra 19:11; “Who is the person who desires life?” → Tehillim 34:13 *Why check:* a check of the four verses about speech — accuracy of the references (Mishlei 18:21; Shemot 23:7; Vayikra 19:11; Tehillim 34:13).
- [ ] `etika-n40` (hard, reform) What is the Chafetz Chaim’s 1876 book “Guarding the Tongue” called? — answer: “Shmirat HaLashon” *Why check:* the title and the year of “Shmirat HaLashon” (1876) — compare with the list of the Chafetz Chaim’s books.
- [ ] `etika-n41` (medium, reform) Speech about a person has nevertheless slipped out. What, according to the lesson, must be done next? *Why check:* CORRECTION OF SPEECH: the lesson directs one to say that the words “were not accurate”; for truthful lashon hara this will not do. Check how to teach teshuvah.
- [ ] `etika-n42` (hard, reform) Before retelling anything about a person, the lesson advises asking oneself four questions. Name them and explain why they cover digital correspondence as well. *Why check:* the four questions before a retelling — a pedagogical note of the lesson, not a quotation from the Chafetz Chaim. Check whether the rabbi wants to keep them as a norm.
- [ ] `etika-n43` (hard, reform) How does the Kitzur Shulchan Aruch (62:9) interpret the word “true” in the commandment about measures? — answer: ““Set a little aside from what is yours and give it to him” — measure generously, a little beyond the measure” *Why check:* KSA 62:9, “set a little aside from what is yours”; se'if 62:10 makes the heap depend on local custom — check whether this is too flat.
- [ ] `etika-n44` (easy, orthodox) Are short weight and short measure permitted in trade with a non-Jew? — answer: “No, they are forbidden in trade with a non-Jew as well” *Why check:* KSA 62:7 — short weight in trade with a non-Jew; an Orthodox detail; check the accuracy.
- [ ] `etika-n45` (hard, reform) What formula does a rabbinical court pronounce over one who does not keep his word (KSA 62:15)? — answer: ““He Who exacted judgment from the generation of the Flood, from the generation of the Tower of Babel, from Sodom, and from the Egyptians, will exact it from the one who does not keep his word”” *Why check:* the long curse formula of KSA 62:15 — compare it word for word with the code.
- [ ] `etika-n46` (medium, reform) What happens to a promise of a gift given to a poor person, according to KSA 62:17? — answer: “It becomes a vow — one may no longer change one’s mind” *Why check:* KSA 62:17 — a promise of a gift to a poor person as a vow; check the accuracy.
- [ ] `etika-n47` (medium, reform) What, according to the commentators, should idolaters say when they see the honest life of Jews? — answer: ““Happy is the people that has such a G-d”” *Why check:* the comment “happy is the people that has such a G-d” — compare with the source.
- [ ] `etika-n48` (easy, orthodox) Is it permitted to mislead a person without monetary loss — for example, to sell non-kosher meat as kosher? — answer: “No, this is forbidden even without monetary loss” *Why check:* KSA 63:4 — the prohibition of deception without monetary loss; an Orthodox detail.
- [ ] `etika-n49` (medium, reform) Delaying the pay of a day laborer who lives on today’s earnings is compared in the tradition with taking a life. — answer: true *Why check:* the comparison of delaying pay with taking a life — a conclusion of the lesson from Devarim 24:15. The earlier wording about 1/6 was removed as inaccurate.
- [ ] `etika-n50` (hard, orthodox) According to the Kitzur Shulchan Aruch (ch. 65), only the lender and the borrower violate the prohibition of interest. — answer: false *Why check:* KSA 65 — the parties to a violation of the prohibition of interest (the scribe, the witnesses, the guarantor) — check.
- [ ] `etika-n51` (hard, reform) Match the ideas of business honesty with their sources. — pairs: Deception in price (ona'ah) → Vayikra 25:14; The prohibition of interest to a fellow Jew → Devarim 23:20–21; “Yes” shall be “yes”: the interpretation of “a true hin” → Vayikra 19:36; “Let all your deeds be for the sake of Heaven” → Pirkei Avot 2:12; “Do not profane My holy Name” → Vayikra 22:32 *Why check:* a check of the five sources of business honesty — accuracy of the verse references.
- [ ] `etika-n52` (easy, reform) What is “profanation of the Name” called — deeds because of which the piety of G-d is called into question? — answer: “Chillul HaShem” *Why check:* the term “chillul HaShem” and the variant spellings — check.
- [ ] `etika-n53` (medium, reform) “Let the hin be true” (Vayikra 19:36) the sages interpret in this way as well: let your “yes” be “yes,” and your “no” be “no.” How will the commandment to keep one’s word look in your work and at home? *Why check:* the interpretation of “hin / hen” stands in KSA 62:17, while 62:16 is about the word and agreement in thought. The lesson cites 62:16. Check the reference.
- [ ] `etika-n54` (easy, reform) Why did Hillel institute the prozbul (Gittin 4:3)? — answer: “So that a debt would be preserved through the seventh year and people would not stop lending to the poor” *Why check:* Hillel’s prozbul (Gittin 4:3) — check the accuracy of the description of the mechanism.
- [ ] `etika-n55` (hard, reform) What new understanding of tikkun olam did the Kabbalah of the Arizal’s teaching (the 16th century) introduce? — answer: “Through mitzvot and good deeds a person returns the world to its original harmony” *Why check:* the historical understanding of the Arizal — compare the wording with the history of ideas.
- [ ] `etika-n56` (hard, reform) What position does the Orthodox tradition take with regard to tikkun olam? — answer: “The main repair is achieved by mitzvot and personal holiness, from which good deeds grow” *Why check:* DIFFERENCE OF MOVEMENTS: the Orthodox emphasis; the differs for Reform and Masorti are taken from the lesson — must be checked.
- [ ] `etika-n57` (easy, reform) The Torah forbids plowing with an ox and a donkey together, taking into account the different nature of the animals (Devarim 22:10). — answer: true *Why check:* the verse Devarim 22:10 — verify the wording and the basis.
- [ ] `etika-n58` (medium, reform) The Kitzur Shulchan Aruch (ch. 190) permits destroying food and things without need, if this does not harm health. — answer: false *Why check:* KSA 190 — the link of bal tashchit with care for the body; check the accuracy.
- [ ] `etika-n59` (medium, reform) According to the Talmud, honoring one’s parents ends together with their life. — answer: false *Why check:* Kiddushin 31b — honoring after the parents’ death; verify the wording.
- [ ] `etika-n60` (hard, reform) Match the commandments about animals with their verses. — pairs: Do not tie shut the mouth of an ox that is chewing → Devarim 25:4; Help even an enemy’s donkey to get up → Shemot 23:5; Do not plow with an ox and a donkey together → Devarim 22:10; So that your ox and your donkey may rest → Shemot 23:12 *Why check:* four verses about animals (Devarim 25:4; Shemot 23:5; Devarim 22:10; Shemot 23:12) — check the references.
- [ ] `etika-n61` (easy, reform) Which in order of the Ten Commandments is the honoring of father and mother? — answer: “the fifth” *Why check:* honoring parents as the fifth of the Ten Commandments — compare with the counting accepted in the lesson (Shemot 20:12).
- [ ] `etika-n62` (medium, reform) How do you understand the prohibition of tza'ar ba'alei chayim, and what are you prepared to observe in daily life? *Why check:* an open question about tza'ar ba'alei chayim — check that the examples do not go beyond the verses of the lesson.
- [ ] `etika-n63` (hard, reform) The Talmud distinguishes “fear” (mora) and “honor” (kavod) of parents. Explain the difference and give examples of how you will show both the one and the other. *Why check:* mora and kavod (Kiddushin 31b) and the mother first (Vayikra 19:3). For a ger, biological parents are a separate question; it is not in the lesson.
<!-- /sec -->
<!-- sec:08-beit-din -->
### Beit din

- [ ] `beit-din-n1` (easy, reform) What did the word “ger” originally mean in the Tanakh? — answer: “A sojourner living among strangers” *Why check:* the meaning of the word “ger” in the Tanakh is a dictionary detail from the lesson text; it is worth checking against the source
- [ ] `beit-din-n2` (medium, reform) From which word, said of the ger in the Torah, do the Sages derive that giyur is performed before a court of three? — answer: “Mishpat” *Why check:* the derivation from the word “mishpat” is a Talmudic interpretation; it is worth checking with a rabbi
- [ ] `beit-din-n3` (hard, reform) What, according to the Rambam (Issurei Biah 13:14), does the beit din ascertain before accepting a candidate? — answer: “Whether he is coming not for the sake of money, status, out of fear, or for the sake of marriage” *Why check:* the exact list of motives to be checked according to the Rambam 13:14 is a source detail
- [ ] `beit-din-n4` (easy, reform) What happens to a ger who, after giyur, has stopped observing the mitzvot? — answer: “He remains a Jew, though an apostate” *Why check:* the formulation “apostate Jew” — Yevamot 47b; check whether this is how one should speak of this at the Reform level
- [ ] `beit-din-n5` (easy, reform) According to the Torah, after giyur the same Torah and the same law apply to the ger as to a Jew by birth. — answer: true *Why check:* the verse about one law is correct, but the scope of the obligation in Reform is different — differs was added
- [ ] `beit-din-n6` (medium, reform) Strict law requires that a candidate for giyur be refused three times before being accepted. — answer: false *Why check:* “three refusals” is a custom (minhag), not a law; it must be checked with a rabbi
- [ ] `beit-din-n7` (medium, reform) Match the term with its meaning. — pairs: Ger tzedek → A righteous ger in the traditional texts; Ger → A person who has undergone giyur (a man); Giyoret → A woman who has undergone giyur; Brit → The covenant of the Almighty with the people of Israel *Why check:* the terminology “ger tzedek” and “ger toshav” — the wordings are worth checking
- [ ] `beit-din-n8` (easy, reform) What is a non-Jew called who lives in the Land of Israel and observes the seven commandments of the descendants of Noach? — answer: “Ger toshav” *Why check:* the definition of “ger toshav” is a detail from the lesson text; check with a rabbi
- [ ] `beit-din-n9` (medium, reform) In your own words, explain: why is the decision about giyur made by the beit din, and not by the candidate himself? *Why check:* a retelling of the meaning of the beit din — the accuracy of the presentation is worth checking
- [ ] `beit-din-n10` (medium, reform) Why is no sacrifice brought at giyur today? What does this change for a man and for a woman? *Why check:* why there is no sacrifice today — check against Issurei Biah 13 and against the fact that, in the Rambam, kabbalat mitzvot is not named as the third rite of Sinai
- [ ] `beit-din-n11` (hard, reform) At what moment, according to the Talmud, is the candidate again told some of the mitzvot? *Why check:* “two Sages” in a court of three — the wording of Yevamot 47b; circumcision before tevilah applies to a man
- [ ] `beit-din-n12` (easy, reform) What is the Conservative movement of Judaism called in Israel and in Europe? — answer: “Masorti” *Why check:* the name “Masorti” is a fact from the lesson; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n13` (easy, reform) What does the candidate receive after completing giyur, in addition to a Jewish name? — answer: “A certificate of giyur” *Why check:* the certificate of giyur is a general fact; it is worth checking
- [ ] `beit-din-n14` (medium, reform) How does the Masorti approach to halacha differ from the Orthodox one? — answer: “Halacha is considered binding, but developing and interpreted by the rabbis of the movement” *Why check:* the Masorti approach to halacha is a difference among the movements; check the wording
- [ ] `beit-din-n15` (hard, orthodox) Who conducts state giyur in Israel? — answer: “Special courts under the Chief Rabbinate” *Why check:* state giyur in Israel is a current detail; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n16` (easy, reform) The Law of Return of 1950 gives every Jew the right to repatriate to Israel. — answer: true *Why check:* the Law of Return is a legal detail; check that it is current
- [ ] `beit-din-n17` (hard, orthodox) The Chief Rabbinate of Israel recognizes all Orthodox batei din abroad, with no exceptions. — answer: false *Why check:* recognition of batei din by the Rabbinate is a current detail; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n18` (easy, reform) Match the organization with its description. — pairs: ROOPI → Association of Progressive Judaism in Russia and the CIS countries; WUPJ → World Union for Progressive Judaism; IMPJ → The Progressive movement that conducts giyur in Israel; Masorti → The Conservative movement in Israel and in Europe *Why check:* the organizations of Progressive Judaism — check the names
- [ ] `beit-din-n19` (medium, orthodox) What is the preparatory program for giyur in Israel mentioned in the lesson called? — answer: “Nativ” *Why check:* the “Nativ” program is a specific detail; check that it is current
- [ ] `beit-din-n20` (easy, reform) In what, according to the lesson, do all the movements of Judaism agree with one another, despite the differences? *Why check:* the agreement of the movements is an overview thesis; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n21` (hard, orthodox) What do you need to find out in advance if the possibility of marrying through the Israeli Rabbinate matters to you? *Why check:* checking a beit din’s status for marriage through the Rabbinate is the norm of the lesson’s Orthodox block; the current standing of recognition changes
- [ ] `beit-din-n22` (medium, reform) How does acceptance of the mitzvot in a Conservative beit din differ from an Orthodox one — and how should one speak about this honestly? *Why check:* Masorti examples (a trip to the synagogue, mixed prayer) — not all Conservative practice is the same
- [ ] `beit-din-n23` (easy, reform) Which of these might one be asked to do at a beit din interview? — answer: “Read a short prayer in Hebrew” *Why check:* the knowledge check at the interview is a detail from the lesson; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n24` (medium, reform) What balance, according to the lesson, is important in an answer about non-Jewish parents? — answer: “Both respect for one’s parents and firmness in one’s own decision” *Why check:* the balance of respect and firmness is practical advice; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n25` (hard, orthodox) What details of practice may be checked in an Orthodox beit din? — answer: “Warming food on Shabbat, muktzeh, and checking eggs for blood” *Why check:* Orthodox details of practice — check against the Kitzur Shulchan Aruch
- [ ] `beit-din-n26` (hard, orthodox) How is one expected to dress for an interview at an Orthodox beit din? — answer: “Modestly: men in a kippah, women in a skirt and with covered shoulders; married women — with the head covered” *Why check:* norms of dress are a community custom; it is worth checking
- [ ] `beit-din-n27` (medium, reform) At the interview it is more convincing to speak about what “Jews are supposed to do,” and not about one’s own practice. — answer: false *Why check:* the style of the answers is advice from the lesson; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n28` (hard, orthodox) In an Orthodox beit din it is enough to say honestly that you do not accept the coming of Mashiach as truth: doubt replaces that acceptance. — answer: false *Why check:* doubt does not replace acceptance of the coming of Mashiach in an Orthodox beit din — a doctrinal formulation
- [ ] `beit-din-n29` (medium, reform) Match the interview situation with the right action. — pairs: A judge’s question is unclear → Calmly ask again; You do not know the answer → Admit it honestly, rather than inventing one; You need to think → Do not be afraid of a pause; They ask about practice → Speak about yourself: “I do” *Why check:* advice on conduct — practical recommendations; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n30` (medium, reform) Which prayer is usually checked at the interview, together with its meaning? — answer: “Shema” *Why check:* checking the Shema is a detail from the lesson; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n31` (medium, reform) What three things does the beit din want to see in a candidate, and how will you show each one? *Why check:* honesty at the interview is a general principle; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n32` (medium, reform) How will you answer questions about Jewish knowledge: what is usually checked, and what should you do if you have forgotten a fact? *Why check:* the list of knowledge that is checked — details from the lesson; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n33` (easy, reform) What does the word “mikveh” mean in translation? — answer: ““A gathering of waters”” *Why check:* the source verse for the laws of the mikveh — a source; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n34` (medium, reform) What else, besides giyur, is a mikveh used for? — answer: “Women immerse according to the laws of family purity, and new utensils are immersed” *Why check:* other uses of the mikveh are details from the lesson; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n35` (medium, reform) How is the presence of natural water arranged in modern mikvaot? — answer: “A reservoir of rainwater is connected to the pool in a special way” *Why check:* the construction of modern mikvaot is a technical detail; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n36` (hard, reform) What is the minimum volume of a kosher mikveh? — answer: “40 se’ah — by different calculations, several hundred liters” *Why check:* 40 se’ah and “several hundred liters” — calculations of the se’ah diverge; the lesson’s source does not name Yoreh De’ah 201
- [ ] `beit-din-n37` (hard, reform) The sea, a natural lake, or a river can never serve as a mikveh. — answer: false *Why check:* natural bodies of water as a mikveh are subtle halacha; check
- [ ] `beit-din-n38` (hard, orthodox) If, during immersion, a lock of hair remained above the water, the immersion still counts once the hair has gotten wet. — answer: false *Why check:* the details of the kashrut of the immersion — check against the Shulchan Aruch
- [ ] `beit-din-n39` (medium, reform) Match the detail of tevilah with what the lesson says about it. — pairs: Second immersion → A custom immediately after the blessing “al ha-tevilah”; Third immersion → This is done in many communities; A mikveh for utensils → Does not replace a kosher mikveh for a person’s tevilah *Why check:* the second and third immersions are a custom, not a condition for the validity of tevilah
- [ ] `beit-din-n40` (easy, reform) What is the pool of natural water called in which one immerses? — answer: “Mikveh” *Why check:* the term “mikveh” — check
- [ ] `beit-din-n41` (medium, reform) You have come out of the mikveh. What words do many people say first, and what does the beit din do with the document and the name? *Why check:* the concept of chatzitzah is a halachic detail; check
